=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given positive integers N, M, K, a non-negative integer C, and an integer sequence A=(A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N) of length N.
Find \displaystyle \sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\min_{1\le i\le N}\lbrace(Ck+A_i)\ \mathrm{mod}\ M \rbrace.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M C K
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \le N \le 10^5
- 1 \le M \le 10^9
- 0 \le C < M
- 1 \le K \le 10^9
- 0 \le A_i < M
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2 5 3 3
1 3

Sample Output 1

4

For k=0, \lbrace(3k+1)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=1 and \lbrace(3k+3)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=3, so \displaystyle \min_{1\le i\le N}\lbrace(Ck+A_i)\ \mathrm{mod}\ M \rbrace=1.
For k=1, \lbrace(3k+1)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=4 and \lbrace(3k+3)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=1, so \displaystyle \min_{1\le i\le N}\lbrace(Ck+A_i)\ \mathrm{mod}\ M \rbrace=1.
For k=2, \lbrace(3k+1)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=2 and \lbrace(3k+3)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=4, so \displaystyle \min_{1\le i\le N}\lbrace(Ck+A_i)\ \mathrm{mod}\ M \rbrace=2.
Therefore, the answer is 1+1+2=4. Hence, print 4.

Sample Input 2

5 4 3 182
0 3 2 1 2

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

5 718 651 193855
3 532 44 109 58

Sample Output 3

29484897

- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's revisit generic prefix_sum for AP interval. Maybe we can implement it with floor_sum using a known algorithm. Let's derive an O(log m) function for count and sum of residues < X.

There is a known technique: To compute sum of (a*i+b) mod m for i=0..n-1, use floor_sum. To compute sum of residues less than X, one can use "floor_sum of floor((a*i+b + m - X)/m)"? Let's search memory internally.

AtCoder Library has floor_sum(n,m,a,b) returns sum_{0<=i<n} floor((a*i+b)/m). There is also "floor_sum" can compute ∑ floor((a*i+b)/m) and ∑ ((a*i+b)%m) maybe. For threshold, there is "sum of floor((a*i+b)/m) for i where (a*i+b)%m < y" maybe.

Let's derive using Euclidean recursion for a function that computes:
F(n, m, a, b, X) = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1} ((a i + b) mod m) * [((a i + b) mod m) < X]
and C similarly.

We can adapt floor_sum recursion. Let's define 0 <= a,b < m maybe, n>=0, 0<=X<=m. We want count C(n,m,a,b,X) and sum S.

Known floor_sum recursion:
floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
  ans=0
  while True:
    if a>=m:
      ans += (n-1)*n*(a//m)//2
      a %= m
    if b>=m:
      ans += n*(b//m)
      b %= m
    y_max = a*n + b
    if y_max < m: break
    n = y_max // m
    b = y_max % m
    m, a = a, m
  return ans

This computes ∑ floor((a i + b)/m). It uses reciprocity.

For threshold count C = F(n,m,a,b+m-X) - F(n,m,a,b) for 0<X<m. This can be computed with floor_sum.

For sum S, maybe we can use a similar recursion with an extra parameter X. Let's attempt to derive.

We want for z_i = a i + b, r_i = z_i mod m. Let's define H(n,m,a,b,X) = ∑ r_i * I(r_i < X). We can split based on a>=m, b>=m:
If a>=m or b>=m, write a = q_a m + a', b = q_b m + b'. Then z_i = m(q_a i + q_b) + (a' i + b'). r_i = (a' i + b') mod m. So H depends only on a',b'. Thus reduce a,b mod m. Good.

So assume 0<=a,b<m.

We need handle X. We can use the same Euclidean transformation as floor_sum. Let y_max = a*n + b (max unrolled value? The standard floor_sum recursion uses the transformation:
floor_sum(n,m,a,b) = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1} floor((a i+b)/m)

Let's attempt.

Let n, m, a, b (0<=a,b<m). Let Y = floor((a*(n-1)+b)/m) + 1 = number of quotient blocks? Actually q_i ranges 0..Q where Q = floor((a(n-1)+b)/m). Sum of r_i for those i in block q: For i from i_start to i_X-1, z_i = q m + r_i, and r_i = a i + b - q m. Since z_i < q m + X, r_i = a i + b - q m. Sum = a * sum_i + (b - q m)*count. This is easy if we know i_start(q), i_X(q). So S_X = ∑_{q=0}^{Q} [ a * (sum of i in [i_start, i_X)) + (b - q m)*count ].

Maybe we can swap summation over q and use floor_sum-like reciprocity. The intervals i_start and i_X are floor/ceil of linear functions in q. We don't need recursive threshold; we can compute S_X using floor_sum over q.

Let's formalize.

Given n, m, a, b with 0<=a<m, 0<=b<m, 0<=X<=m. Let z_i = a i + b for i=0..n-1. Let Q = floor((a*(n-1)+b)/m) (max quotient). q ranges 0..Q. For each q, block of i with q_i = q is i ∈ [L_q, R_q) where:
L_q = min i such that z_i >= q m = ceil((q m - b)/a), clamped to [0,n].
R_q = min i such that z_i >= (q+1)m = ceil(((q+1)m - b)/a), clamped to [0,n].
For q=Q, R_Q = n (since no (Q+1)m maybe; formula gives >n). Actually define R_q = min(n, ceil(((q+1)m - b)/a)).
For q < Q, R_q = L_{q+1}. Good.

Within block q, r_i < X iff z_i < q m + X, i.e. i < T_q where T_q = min i such that z_i >= q m + X = ceil((q m + X - b)/a). Since X<=m, T_q <= R_q (for X=m, T_q = ceil(((q+1)m - b)/a)=R_q; for X=0, T_q = L_q). Clamp T_q to [L_q, R_q]? If X=0, T_q = L_q. If X=m, T_q=R_q. For 0<X<m, T_q between L_q and R_q. Thus selected i in block q: i ∈ [L_q, T_q) (if T_q > L_q). Count c_q = T_q - L_q. Sum of i s_q = T_q(T_q-1)/2 - L_q(L_q-1)/2. Sum of r_i = a*s_q + (b - q m)*c_q.

Therefore:
C_X = ∑_{q=0}^{Q} (T_q - L_q).
S_X = a * ∑_{q=0}^{Q} (T_q(T_q-1)/2 - L_q(L_q-1)/2) + ∑_{q=0}^{Q} (b - q m)(T_q - L_q).

Now L_q and T_q are ceil of linear functions:
L_q = ceil((m q - b)/a) = floor((m q - b + a - 1)/a). Let B_L = a - 1 - b (since -b + a -1). L_q = floor((m q + B_L)/a), clamped to [0,n]. For q=0, if b? z_0=b. q=0 block starts at i=0 always? L_0 = ceil(-b/a) = 0 (if b>=0). Formula floor((0 - b + a -1)/a). If b=0, floor((a-1)/a)=0. If b>0, numerator a-1-b could be negative, floor negative? Example a=10,b=3: floor((7)/10)=0. b=15? but b<m, a could less than b? b<m, a maybe 3,b=10? after mod a<m, b can be >a. Example a=3,b=10,m=20: L_0=ceil(-10/3)= -3? But i cannot negative; min i with z_i>=0 is 0. Formula floor((0-10+2)/3)=floor(-8/3)=-3, not 0. Need clamp to 0. So L_q = max(0, floor((m q + a -1 - b)/a)). But for q>=1, numerator likely nonnegative? Not necessarily if b large. We can clamp.

T_q = ceil((m q + X - b)/a) = floor((m q + X - b + a -1)/a) = floor((m q + B_T)/a) with B_T = X - b + a -1. Clamp to [0,n].

But summing floor of linear functions over q=0..Q can be done with floor_sum. However T_q and L_q also appear squared in sum of i. Need sum of T_q^2 and L_q^2. Floor_sum only gives sum of floor, not square. We can compute sum of squares of floor((m q + B)/a) using a generalized floor_sum that returns sum of floor and floor^2 maybe. Or derive formula using floor_sum of floor and maybe sum of q*floor? There is known extension: floor_sum can compute ∑ floor((a*i+b)/m), ∑ i*floor, ∑ floor^2 using recursive algorithm. We can implement a generalized floor_sum returning these sums. That might be the robust solution.

Alternatively, maybe we can avoid sum of i by computing S_X directly with floor_sum of T_q and L_q plus q*T_q/q*L_q. Let's expand:

C = ∑ T_q - ∑ L_q.

S = a/2 ∑ (T_q^2 - T_q - L_q^2 + L_q) + b C - m ∑ q(T_q - L_q).

Need:

Then S can be computed. This is more complex but doable with a generalized floor_sum that returns sum of floor, i*floor, floor^2 maybe.

Let's consider using the block decomposition over q for the interval directly, not prefix_sum. For interval [l,r) in x (0<=l<r<=m), condition l <= r_i < r. In each quotient block q, z_i = q m + r_i. But perhaps there is an even simpler way: Since N=1e5, M' up to 1e9, K up to 1e9. We can use the "floor_sum" from AtCoder Library which in Python can compute floor_sum in O(log m). We can extend it to compute sum of floor and floor^2 and i*floor. Let's derive the recursive formulas.

Standard floor_sum:
def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans

This computes ∑ floor((a*i+b)/m) for i=0..n-1, with a,b can be >=m initially.

For generalized sums, we can write a recursive function that returns (sum_f, sum_i_f, sum_f2) maybe. Let's derive from the standard recursion.

Let S0(n,m,a,b) = ∑ f_i where f_i = floor((a i + b)/m).
S1(n,m,a,b) = ∑ i f_i.
S2(n,m,a,b) = ∑ f_i^2.

We can reduce a,b modulo m:
If a = q_a m + a', b = q_b m + b', then f_i = q_a i + q_b + floor((a' i + b')/m) = q_a i + q_b + f'_i.
Then:
S0 = q_a * n(n-1)/2 + q_b * n + S0'.
S1 = ∑ i(q_a i + q_b + f'_i) = q_a * n(n-1)(2n-1)/6 + q_b * n(n-1)/2 + S1'.
S2 = ∑ (q_a i + q_b + f'_i)^2. Expand:
= q_a^2 ∑ i^2 + q_b^2 n + ∑ f'^2 + 2 q_a q_b ∑ i + 2 q_a ∑ i f' + 2 q_b ∑ f'.
So can compute from S0',S1',S2'.

Then Euclidean swap: For 0<=a,b<m. Let y_max = a*n + b. If y_max < m, all f_i=0, so S0=S1=S2=0. Otherwise, standard floor_sum transforms by setting n' = y_max // m, b' = y_max % m, and swapping (m,a) -> (a,m)? Actually the recursion uses:
floor_sum(n, m, a, b) = (n-1)*? Let's recall exact identity.

For 0<=a,b<m, let y_max = a*n + b. If y_max >= m:
Let n' = y_max // m, b' = y_max % m.

floor((a i + b)/m) = number of integers j>=1 such that m j <= a i + b.

Let's use known AtCoder floor_sum recursion formula:
floor_sum(n, m, a, b) returns ans.
Pseudo from ACL:
while:
  if a>=m: ans += (n-1)*n*(a//m)//2; a%=m
  if b>=m: ans += n*(b//m); b%=m
  y_max = a*n + b
  if y_max < m: break

  b = y_max % m

This is iterative but can be converted to recursive:

Maybe there is a known generalized floor_sum implementation. Let's search memory: AtCoder Library internal floor_sum can compute "floor_sum" only. There is a Python snippet for "floor_sum of floor, i*floor, floor^2" using recursion:

def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans

Let's formalize gfs(n, m, a, b):
Returns (sum f, sum i*f, sum f^2) for f_i = floor((a*i+b)/m), i=0..n-1.

We can implement recursively:
def gfs(n,m,a,b):
    if n<=0: return (0,0,0)
    # reduce a,b
    if a>=m or b>=m:
        qa, a = divmod(a,m)
        qb, b = divmod(b,m)
        g0,g1,g2 = gfs(n,m,a,b)
        S1 = n*(n-1)//2
        S2 = n*(n-1)*(2*n-1)//6
        s0 = qa*S1 + qb*n + g0
        s1 = qa*S2 + qb*S1 + g1
        s2 = qa*qa*S2 + qb*qb*n + g2 + 2*qa*qb*S1 + 2*qa*g1 + 2*qb*g0
        return s0,s1,s2
    # now 0<=a,b<m
    y = a*n + b
    if y < m:
        return (0,0,0)
    n2 = y // m
    b2 = y % m
    H0,H1,H2 = gfs(n2, a, m, b2)  # m_new=a, a_new=m
    s0 = H0
    s1 = (2*n*H0 - H0 - H2)//2
    s2 = (2*n2 - 1)*H0 - 2*H1
    return s0,s1,s2

Need verify reduction formulas with s1/s2 when a,b reduced. Let's test with a>=m example.

gfs(3,5,7,1): qa=1,a=2; qb=0,b=1. g=gfs(3,5,2,1) = (1,2,1). S1=3,S2=5.
s0=1*3+0+1=4.
s1=1*5+0+2=7.
s2=1*5+0+1+0+2*1*2+0=5+1+4=10. good.

If b>=m: n=3,m=5,a=2,b=6. f: floor6/5=1,8/5=1,10/5=2 sum4, s1=0+1+4=5, s2=1+1+4=6.
qa=0,a=2; qb=1,b=1. g=(1,2,1). s0=0+3+1=4. s1=0+1*3+2=5. s2=0+1*3+1+0+0+2*1*1=3+1+2=6. good.

Let's implement a helper:
def floor_range_sum(count, denom, coeff, offset, start):
    # sum_{p=0}^{count-1} floor((coeff*(p+start)+offset)/denom)? Wait if q=p+start, value = floor((coeff*q + offset)/denom). We can pass offset as B, and start.
    # We want floor((coeff*(p+start)+B)/denom) = floor((coeff*p + coeff*start + B)/denom).
    # gfs(count, denom, coeff, coeff*start+B) returns s0,s1,s2 for p.
    if count <=0: return (0,0,0)
    s0,s1,s2 = gfs(count, denom, coeff, coeff*start + B)
    sum_qV = s1 + start*s0
    return s0, sum_qV, s2
But note gfs signature (n, m, a, b) computes floor((a*i+b)/m). Here denom=a_old, coeff=m_old. So call gfs(count, denom, coeff, offset). Yes.

Now compute L_sum over q=1..Q:

But wait if Q=0 count=0. For Q>0, offset = m*1 + B = m + a -1 - b, positive. Good.

T0 special:

T0_s0 = T0

T0_s2 = T0*T0

T_Q special:
if Q >= 0? There is always q=Q. But if Q=0, T_Q is same as T0? We already included T0 for q=0. If we also add T_Q for Q=0, double count. So handle q=Q only if Q>0? Actually q ranges 0..Q. We split:
- q=0: T0
- q=1..Q-1: mid
- q=Q: last
If Q=0, only q=0, so no last. If Q=1, mid count=0, last q=1.
So if Q >= 1:
rawQ = ceil((Q*m + X - b)/a) = (Q*m + X - b + a -1)//a (numerator positive since Q>=1)

TQ_s0 = TQ
TQ_s1_q = Q * TQ
TQ_s2 = TQ*TQ
If Q=0, no separate.

Now combine T sums:
T_s0 = T0_s0 + T_mid_s0 + (TQ_s0 if Q>=1 else 0)

T_s2 = T0_s2 + T_mid_s2 + (TQ_s2 if Q>=1 else 0)

L sums:

Original problem:
Given N,M,C,K,A. Need sum_{k=0}^{K-1} min_i (Ck + A_i mod M).

We transformed:
g = gcd(C,M)
M' = M // g
C' = C // g (coprime to M')
For each A_i, q_i = A_i // g, r_i = A_i % g.
We need sum over u_k = (-C' * k) mod M' for k=0..K-1 of D(u) = g * δ(u) + ρ(u), where δ(u) = distance to next q in set Q (unique q with min r? Actually Q set of q_i, with weight r_min(q) = min r_i among A_i with that q). Need compute sum over AP of δ and ρ.

But maybe we can compute sum of D(u) directly using prefix_sum on original A? Wait original D(u) = min_i (r_i + g * ((q_i - u) mod M')). We argued nearest q dominates, so D = g*δ + r_next. Need prove and implement.

Let's verify with original formula and scaling. For any u, δ = min_i ((q_i - u) mod M'). Let i* achieve minimal δ. Then D(u) = r_i* + g δ. Since for any other j with δ_j > δ, r_j + g δ_j >= 0 + g(δ+1) > (g-1)+gδ >= r_i*+gδ. If δ_j = δ, choose min r among those. So yes.

Thus we need:
Q positions: unique q values. For each q, weight w(q) = min r_i among A_i with q_i=q.
For u in [0,M'-1], δ(u) = distance from u to next q clockwise on circle of circumference M'. Let next_q(u) be that q. ρ(u) = w(next_q(u)) (if u exactly at q, next=q). Then D(u)=g*δ(u)+w(next_q(u)).

Now sum over u_k = (-C' k mod M'), k=0..K-1. Since C' coprime to M', the sequence u_k is an arithmetic progression modulo M' with step d = (-C') mod M', gcd(d,M')=1. We can use our prefix_sum machinery to sum δ(u_k) and w(next_q(u_k)) over AP.

First, δ(u) is exactly distance to next point in Q on circle size M'. This is the same as original d(t) but with point set Q and no weights. We can compute sum δ over AP by summing over gaps between Q points:
For each gap [L, R) in Q (cyclic, R = next q after L, length len = R-L, with wrap len = q0 + M' - q_last), for u in this gap, δ(u) = R - u (with wrap lifted). Contribution = ∑_{u in AP ∩ gap} (R - u_lifted). This requires count and sum of u in interval. We can use prefix_count and prefix_sum for u sequence.

But now step d is coprime to M', so in prefix_sum function, a = d, b = u_0 = 0? Wait u_k = (u0 + d*k) mod M'. Here u0 = 0 (since k=0 -> -0 mod M'=0), d = (-C') mod M'. So sequence r_i = (a*i + b) mod m with a=d, b=0, n=K, m=M'. Good. gcd(d,M')=1 but prefix_sum works general.

def interval_stats(n,m,a,b,l,r): # a step, b start, n length
    # count and sum of residues in [l,r)
    if l>=r or n<=0: return (0,0)
    # Use prefix_sum:
    cnt_r, sum_r = prefix_sum(n,m,a,b,r)
    cnt_l, sum_l = prefix_sum(n,m,a,b,l)
    return cnt_r-cnt_l, sum_r-sum_l

Let's write a function prefix_sum(n, m, a, b, X):
# Returns (count, sum) of residues (a*i+b)%m < X, for i=0..n-1.
# Assumes 0<=a,b<m? We can reduce a,b modulo m at start? The sequence residues depend on a mod m and b mod m. We can set a %= m, b %= m.
# If n==0: return (0,0)
# If X<=0: return (0,0)
# If X>=m: count=n, sum = total_sum_residues.
# total_sum_residues = a*n*(n-1)//2 + b*n - m*gfs(n,m,a,b)[0]
# If a==0: constant b.
# Else 0<X<m:
# Q = (a*(n-1)+b)//m
# Compute as above.
Return count, sum.

- __init__(n,m,a,b):
   self.n=n; self.m=m; self.a=a%m; self.b=b%m
   if n==0: ...
   if self.a==0: no precompute.
   else:

      if self.Q > 0:

          self.L_s0, self.L_s1q, self.L_s2 = floor_range_sum(self.Q, self.a, self.m, B_L, 1)
      else zeros.

       TQ = min(n, rawQ)

   S = a * sum_i_diff + b * C - m * sum_q_diff

Now, floor_range_sum uses gfs. Let's implement it carefully:
def floor_range_sum(count, denom, coeff, B, start):
    # sum_{p=0}^{count-1} floor((coeff*(p+start)+B)/denom)
    if count <= 0:
        return (0,0,0)
    offset = coeff * start + B
    # call gfs(count, denom, coeff, offset)
    s0, s1, s2 = gfs(count, denom, coeff, offset)
    sum_q = s1 + start * s0
    return s0, sum_q, s2
Here denom = a (old a), coeff = m (old m). offset may be large but nonnegative. In L_sum, B_L = a -1 - b. For start=1, offset = m + B_L = m + a -1 - b. Since b < m, offset >= a >=1, nonnegative. For T_mid, B_T = X + a -1 - b. start=1, offset = m + X + a -1 - b. Since b<m, X>=1, offset >= m +1 + a -1 - (m-1) = a+1? positive. Good.

Need ensure gfs handles offset maybe >= denom? Yes.

Initialize delta_sum=0, rho_sum=0.

For i in range(P-1):
    L = qs[i]; R = qs[i+1]; w = weights[i+1]
    cnt = pref_cnt[i+1] - pref_cnt[i]  # interval [L+1, R+1)
    sumu = pref_sum[i+1] - pref_sum[i]
    delta_sum += cnt * R - sumu
    rho_sum += cnt * w
Because R is next q, and δ=R-u. At u=R, δ=0; formula cnt*R - sumu includes u=R with δ=0. Good.

Wrap:
    L = qs[-1]; R0 = qs[0]; w0 = weights[0]
    # part1 [L+1, M)
    cnt1 = total_cnt - pref_cnt[-1]
    sum1 = total_sum - pref_sum[-1]

    rho_sum += cnt1 * w0
    # part2 [0, R0+1)
    cnt2 = pref_cnt[0]  # prefix at R0+1
    sum2 = pref_sum[0]
    delta_sum += cnt2 * R0 - sum2
    rho_sum += cnt2 * w0

Now, gfs implementation details and potential recursion depth.

Let's write gfs:

def gfs(n, m, a, b):
    # returns (s0, s1, s2) for sum floor((a*i+b)/m), i=0..n-1
    if n <= 0:
        return (0,0,0)
    if a >= m or b >= m:
        qa, a = divmod(a, m)
        qb, b = divmod(b, m)
        g0, g1, g2 = gfs(n, m, a, b)
        S1 = n*(n-1)//2
        S2 = n*(n-1)*(2*n-1)//6
        s0 = qa*S1 + qb*n + g0
        s1 = qa*S2 + qb*S1 + g1
        s2 = qa*qa*S2 + qb*qb*n + g2 + 2*qa*qb*S1 + 2*qa*g1 + 2*qb*g0
        return s0,s1,s2
    # now 0<=a,b<m
    y = a*n + b
    if y < m:
        return (0,0,0)
    n2 = y // m
    b2 = y % m
    H0,H1,H2 = gfs(n2, a, m, b2)  # note m_param=a, a_param=m
    s0 = H0
    s1 = (2*n*H0 - H0 - H2)//2
    s2 = (2*n2 - 1)*H0 - 2*H1
    return s0,s1,s2

Now, let's implement final code.

Pseudo:

import sys, math
sys.setrecursionlimit(1_000_000)

def gfs(n,m,a,b):
    if n <= 0:
        return (0,0,0)
    if a >= m or b >= m:
        qa, a = divmod(a, m)
        qb, b = divmod(b, m)
        g0,g1,g2 = gfs(n,m,a,b)
        S1 = n*(n-1)//2
        S2 = n*(n-1)*(2*n-1)//6
        s0 = qa*S1 + qb*n + g0
        s1 = qa*S2 + qb*S1 + g1
        s2 = qa*qa*S2 + qb*qb*n + g2 + 2*qa*qb*S1 + 2*qa*g1 + 2*qb*g0
        return s0,s1,s2
    y = a*n + b
    if y < m:
        return (0,0,0)
    n2 = y // m
    b2 = y % m
    H0,H1,H2 = gfs(n2, a, m, b2)
    s0 = H0
    s1 = (2*n*H0 - H0 - H2)//2
    s2 = (2*n2 - 1)*H0 - 2*H1
    return s0,s1,s2

def floor_range_sum(count, denom, coeff, B, start):
    if count <= 0:
        return (0,0,0)
    offset = coeff * start + B
    s0,s1,s2 = gfs(count, denom, coeff, offset)
    sum_q = s1 + start * s0
    return s0, sum_q, s2

class PrefixCounter:
    def __init__(self, n, m, a, b):
        self.n = n
        self.m = m
        self.a = a % m if m else a
        self.b = b % m if m else b
        self.total_cnt = n
        self.total_sum = 0
        if n == 0:
            self.Q = 0
            self.L_s0 = self.L_s1q = self.L_s2 = 0
            return
        if m == 0: ... not possible
        # total sum
        if self.a == 0:
            self.total_sum = self.b * n
        else:
            s0,_,_ = gfs(n,m,self.a,self.b)
            self.total_sum = self.a * n*(n-1)//2 + self.b*n - m*s0
        if self.a == 0:
            self.Q = 0
            self.L_s0 = self.L_s1q = self.L_s2 = 0
        else:
            self.Q = (self.a * (n-1) + self.b) // m
            if self.Q > 0:
                B_L = self.a - 1 - self.b
                self.L_s0, self.L_s1q, self.L_s2 = floor_range_sum(self.Q, self.a, self.m, B_L, 1)
            else:
                self.L_s0 = self.L_s1q = self.L_s2 = 0

    def prefix(self, X):
        n=self.n; m=self.m; a=self.a; b=self.b
        if n == 0 or X <= 0:
            return (0,0)
        if X >= m:
            return (n, self.total_sum)
        if a == 0:
            if b < X:
                return (n, n*b)
            else:
                return (0,0)
        # 0 < X < m
        # T0
        if X <= b:
            raw0 = 0
        else:
            raw0 = (X - b + a - 1) // a
        if raw0 > n:
            raw0 = n
        T0 = raw0
        T_s0 = T0
        T_s1q = 0
        T_s2 = T0 * T0
        Q = self.Q
        if Q >= 2:
            count = Q - 1
            B_T = X + a - 1 - b
            mid_s0, mid_s1q, mid_s2 = floor_range_sum(count, a, m, B_T, 1)
            T_s0 += mid_s0
            T_s1q += mid_s1q
            T_s2 += mid_s2
        if Q >= 1:
            rawQ = (Q * m + X - b + a - 1) // a
            if rawQ > n:
                rawQ = n
            TQ = rawQ
            T_s0 += TQ
            T_s1q += Q * TQ
            T_s2 += TQ * TQ
        L_s0 = self.L_s0; L_s1q = self.L_s1q; L_s2 = self.L_s2
        C = T_s0 - L_s0
        sum_i_diff = (T_s2 - T_s0 - (L_s2 - L_s0)) // 2
        sum_q_diff = T_s1q - L_s1q
        S = a * sum_i_diff + b * C - m * sum_q_diff
        return C, S

Need check if Q=0 and T0 maybe >n? We clamped raw0 to n. Good. If Q=0, L_s0=0. C=T0. sum_i_diff=(T_s2-T_s0)//2. S formula works.

But wait if Q=0, there is no wrap in sequence, but T0 is number of initial i with r<X before first wrap. Since no wrap within n, all selected i are in first block, count=T0. Good.

If Q>=1, T0 clamped to n but should also be <= L_1. Since X<m, raw0 <= ceil((m-1-b)/a) <= L_1? If b maybe m-1, raw0=0. If X=m-1 and b=0, raw0=ceil((m-1)/a) <= ceil(m/a)=L_1. So okay. min n not harmful.

Now, total_sum in __init__: if a=0, gfs not needed. If a>0, gfs s0. But if n=0, total_sum=0. We handle.

Now, main solve:

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N,M,C,K = map(int,input().split())
    A = list(map(int,input().split()))
    g = math.gcd(C,M)
    M1 = M // g
    if M1 == 1:
        print(K * min(A))
        return
    C1 = C // g
    d = (-C1) % M1
    # build q->min r
    mp = {}
    for a_val in A:
        q = a_val // g
        r = a_val % g
        if q not in mp or r < mp[q]:
            mp[q] = r
    qs = sorted(mp.keys())
    weights = [mp[q] for q in qs]
    P = len(qs)
    # PrefixCounter for sequence u_k = (d*k + 0) mod M1, length K
    pc = PrefixCounter(K, M1, d, 0)
    total_cnt = K
    total_sum = pc.total_sum
    # precompute prefix at X = q+1 for each q
    pref_cnt = []
    pref_sum = []
    for q in qs:
        X = q + 1
        c, s = pc.prefix(X)
        pref_cnt.append(c)
        pref_sum.append(s)
    delta_sum = 0
    rho_sum = 0
    # non-wrap gaps
    for i in range(P-1):
        L = qs[i]
        R = qs[i+1]
        w = weights[i+1]
        cnt = pref_cnt[i+1] - pref_cnt[i]
        sumu = pref_sum[i+1] - pref_sum[i]
        delta_sum += cnt * R - sumu
        rho_sum += cnt * w
    # wrap gap
    L = qs[-1]
    R0 = qs[0]
    w0 = weights[0]
    # part1 [L+1, M1)
    cnt1 = total_cnt - pref_cnt[-1]
    sum1 = total_sum - pref_sum[-1]
    delta_sum += cnt1 * (R0 + M1) - sum1
    rho_sum += cnt1 * w0
    # part2 [0, R0+1)
    cnt2 = pref_cnt[0]  # prefix at R0+1
    sum2 = pref_sum[0]
    delta_sum += cnt2 * R0 - sum2
    rho_sum += cnt2 * w0
    ans = g * delta_sum + rho_sum
    print(ans)

Let's examine the prefix_sum derivation one more time for correctness with n arbitrary and a,b reduced.

We used quotient block decomposition:
For each i, z_i = a i + b, q_i = floor(z_i/m), r_i = z_i mod m.
Blocks q=0..Q where Q = floor((a(n-1)+b)/m). For each q, block i ∈ [L_q, R_q) where L_q = min i with z_i >= q m, R_q = min i with z_i >= (q+1)m (or n for q=Q). Since z_i increasing, L_q = ceil((q m - b)/a). For q=0, L_0=0. For q>=1, formula.
Within block, r_i < X iff z_i < q m + X, so i < T_q where T_q = min i with z_i >= q m + X = ceil((q m + X - b)/a). Since X<=m, T_q <= R_q. For X=m, T_q=R_q; for X=0, T_q=L_q. We handle 0<X<m.
Then selected i in block q: [L_q, T_q). Count T_q-L_q. Sum r_i = ∑ (z_i - q m) = a∑i + (b - q m)*count. Sum i over [L,T) = (T(T-1)-L(L-1))/2. We compute sums over q using floor sums.

We computed L_q for q=1..Q via floor((m q + a -1 - b)/a). This equals ceil((m q - b)/a). Let's verify for q>=1 and b maybe large. ceil(x/a) = floor((x + a -1)/a) for integer x. x = m q - b. So floor((m q - b + a -1)/a). Yes. For q=0, x=-b, ceil(-b/a) could be <=0, but L_0=0. We set L_0=0. For q>=1, x>0 because m q - b >= m - b >0 (since b<m), so formula positive. Good.

T_q = ceil((q m + X - b)/a) = floor((q m + X - b + a -1)/a). For q=0, x=X-b may be negative; T_0 should be max(0, ceil(x/a)). We handle separately. For q>=1, x >0? q m + X - b >= m + X - b. Since b<m, m-b>=1, X>=1, so >=2? Actually if X=1,b=m-1, m+1-(m-1)=2>0. So positive. Good. For q=Q, T_Q may exceed n because block q=Q ends at n (not at (Q+1)m). We clamp to n. For q<Q, T_q <= R_q = L_{q+1} <= n. We compute q=1..Q-1 via floor formula without clamp; should be <=n. Let's verify if q=Q-1 and X=m-1, T_{Q-1} <= R_{Q-1}=L_Q <= n. Since X<m, yes. Good.

L_sum for q=1..Q via floor_range_sum count Q. If Q=0 none.
T_mid for q=1..Q-1 via floor_range_sum count Q-1. If Q=1 none.
T_Q for q=Q if Q>=1.
T_0 for q=0.

What about q=0 if Q>=1 and T0 formula raw0 = ceil((X-b)/a) max 0. But if X-b negative, T0=0. If X-b positive, raw0 could be > L_1? We argued no. Let's prove: L_1 = ceil((m-b)/a). Since X <= m-1, X-b <= m-1-b < m-b, so ceil((X-b)/a) <= ceil((m-b)/a) = L_1. If X-b negative, 0 <= L_1. So T0<=L_1. Good.

Now, sum_i_diff formula:
∑ T(T-1)/2 = (∑T^2 - ∑T)/2. We compute T_s2 - T_s0. Is it always even? Yes because each T(T-1) even. Similarly L. We combine (T_s2 - T_s0 - (L_s2 - L_s0))//2. Since both differences even, difference even. Good.

Now, gfs s1/s2 formulas in swap. Let's re-derive s2 formula to ensure no off-by-one:
s2 = ∑ f_i^2. f_i = ∑_{j=1}^{f_i} 1. f_i^2 = ∑_{j=1}^{f_i} (2j-1). Then s2 = ∑_{j=1}^{n2} (2j-1) * #{i: f_i >= j}. We used # = h_{n2-j}. Need verify for j=n2, h_0 = n - L_{n2}? L_{n2} = min i with f_i >= n2. Since f_i <= n2? Could f_i = n2+? Let's check max f_{n-1} = floor((a(n-1)+b)/m). n2 = floor((a n + b)/m). Difference max f_{n-1} <= n2? Since a(n-1)+b = a n + b - a = m n2 + b2 - a. If b2 >= a, max f = n2; else n2-1. So f_i <= n2. Thus j ranges 1..n2. # with f_i >= j = n - L_j. L_j = ceil((m j - b)/a). h_{n2-j} = floor((m(n2-j)+b2)/a). We derived L_j = n - h_{n2-j}. Let's verify for j=n2: h_0 = floor(b2/a). L_{n2} = ceil((m n2 - b)/a). Since m n2 = a n + b - b2, m n2 - b = a n - b2. L = ceil((a n - b2)/a) = n + ceil(-b2/a) = n - floor(b2/a) = n - h_0. Good. So # = h_{n2-j}. Then s2 = ∑_{t=0}^{n2-1} (2(n2-t)-1) h_t = (2n2-1)H0 - 2H1. Correct.

s1 derivation: s1 = ∑_{j=1}^{n2} ∑_{i=L_j}^{n-1} i = ∑ (S_total - L_j(L_j-1)/2). L_j = n - h_{n2-j}. We derived s1 = n H0 - (H0+H2)/2. Let's re-derive quickly:
∑ L_j(L_j-1)/2 = ∑_{t=0}^{n2-1} (n-h_t)(n-h_t-1)/2
= 1/2 ∑ (n^2 -2nh_t + h_t^2 - n + h_t)
= 1/2 [ n2 n(n-1) -2n H0 + H2 + H0 ].
s1 = n2 * n(n-1)/2 - that = [ n2 n(n-1) - n2 n(n-1) + 2nH0 - H2 - H0 ]/2 = nH0 - (H0+H2)/2. Correct.

Recursive gfs:
def gfs(n,m,a,b):
    if n<=0: return zeros
    if a>=m or b>=m:
       reduce; recurse; combine; return
    y=a*n+b
    if y<m: return zeros
    recurse swap; combine; return

We can implement iterative by pushing frames:
stack = []
while True:
    if n<=0: res=(0,0,0); break
    if a>=m or b>=m:
        qa,a=divmod(a,m); qb,b=divmod(b,m)
        stack.append(('reduce', qa,qb,n,m))
        continue
    y=a*n+b
    if y<m:
        res=(0,0,0); break
    n2=y//m; b2=y%m
    stack.append(('swap', n,m,a,b? need original n,m,a,b for combine))
    n,m,a,b = n2, a, m, b2
    continue
Then unwind. But reduction after reduction may need combine with previous. This is doable but more complex. Recursive is simpler and likely okay. AtCoder Python recursion with 3e6 calls might be okay? 3e6 function calls could be ~1s? Actually Python function call ~0.1us? No, ~0.1-0.3 micro? More like 0.1-0.2 us? 3e6 ~0.3-0.6s? Maybe 1-2s. Plus other work. Should be okay in PyPy maybe.

But note each gfs call may recurse multiple times? It recurses once per level. 1e5 prefix calls * depth maybe 20 = 2e6 calls. Fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if in main, if q0=M1-1 and P=1, part1 empty, part2 all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if in main, if `M1` is 1 direct, but if `K` is 1 and minA maybe 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if in main, if `C` is 0 and `M` is 1, direct K*0=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if in main, if `N` is 1e5 and `data` list memory, okay.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if in main, if `A` list values are read and `min(A)` for direct; if A list empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
requires final code in code block. Need include code only. Ensure no extra text.</think>```python
import sys
import math

sys.setrecursionlimit(1_000_000)


def gfs(n, m, a, b):
    """
    Returns (s0, s1, s2):
      s0 = sum floor((a*i+b)/m)
      s1 = sum i * floor((a*i+b)/m)
      s2 = sum floor((a*i+b)/m)^2
    for i = 0..n-1.
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return (0, 0, 0)

    if a >= m or b >= m:
        qa, a = divmod(a, m)
        qb, b = divmod(b, m)
        g0, g1, g2 = gfs(n, m, a, b)

        S1 = n * (n - 1) // 2
        S2 = n * (n - 1) * (2 * n - 1) // 6

        s0 = qa * S1 + qb * n + g0
        s1 = qa * S2 + qb * S1 + g1
        s2 = (qa * qa * S2 + qb * qb * n + g2 +
              2 * qa * qb * S1 +
              2 * qa * g1 +
              2 * qb * g0)
        return s0, s1, s2

    y = a * n + b
    if y < m:
        return (0, 0, 0)

    n2 = y // m
    b2 = y % m
    H0, H1, H2 = gfs(n2, a, m, b2)

    s0 = H0
    s1 = (2 * n * H0 - H0 - H2) // 2
    s2 = (2 * n2 - 1) * H0 - 2 * H1
    return s0, s1, s2


def floor_range_sum(count, denom, coeff, B, start):
    """
    sum_{p=0}^{count-1} floor((coeff*(p+start)+B)/denom)
    Returns (sum_value, sum_q_value, sum_value^2),
    where q = p + start.
    """
    if count <= 0:
        return (0, 0, 0)

    offset = coeff * start + B
    s0, s1, s2 = gfs(count, denom, coeff, offset)
    sum_q = s1 + start * s0
    return s0, sum_q, s2


class PrefixCounter:
    """
    For sequence r_i = (a*i+b) mod m, i=0..n-1,
    answers count and sum of residues < X.
    """

    def __init__(self, n, m, a, b):
        self.n = n
        self.m = m
        self.a = a % m
        self.b = b % m
        self.total_cnt = n
        self.total_sum = 0

        if n == 0:
            self.Q = 0
            self.L_s0 = self.L_s1q = self.L_s2 = 0
            return

        if self.a == 0:
            self.total_sum = self.b * n
            self.Q = 0
            self.L_s0 = self.L_s1q = self.L_s2 = 0
            return

        _, s0, _ = gfs(n, m, self.a, self.b)
        self.total_sum = (self.a * n * (n - 1) // 2 +
                          self.b * n - m * s0)

        self.Q = (self.a * (n - 1) + self.b) // m

        if self.Q > 0:
            B_L = self.a - 1 - self.b
            self.L_s0, self.L_s1q, self.L_s2 = floor_range_sum(
                self.Q, self.a, self.m, B_L, 1
            )
        else:
            self.L_s0 = self.L_s1q = self.L_s2 = 0

    def prefix(self, X):
        n = self.n
        m = self.m
        a = self.a
        b = self.b

        if n == 0 or X <= 0:
            return (0, 0)
        if X >= m:
            return (n, self.total_sum)

        if a == 0:
            if b < X:
                return (n, n * b)
            return (0, 0)

        # q = 0 block
        if X <= b:
            raw0 = 0
        else:
            raw0 = (X - b + a - 1) // a
        if raw0 > n:
            raw0 = n
        T0 = raw0

        T_s0 = T0
        T_s1q = 0
        T_s2 = T0 * T0

        Q = self.Q

        # q = 1 .. Q-1
        if Q >= 2:
            count = Q - 1
            B_T = X + a - 1 - b
            mid_s0, mid_s1q, mid_s2 = floor_range_sum(
                count, a, m, B_T, 1
            )
            T_s0 += mid_s0
            T_s1q += mid_s1q
            T_s2 += mid_s2

        # q = Q
        if Q >= 1:
            rawQ = (Q * m + X - b + a - 1) // a
            if rawQ > n:
                rawQ = n
            TQ = rawQ
            T_s0 += TQ
            T_s1q += Q * TQ
            T_s2 += TQ * TQ

        L_s0 = self.L_s0
        L_s1q = self.L_s1q
        L_s2 = self.L_s2

        cnt = T_s0 - L_s0

        sum_i_diff = (T_s2 - T_s0 - (L_s2 - L_s0)) // 2
        sum_q_diff = T_s1q - L_s1q

        s = a * sum_i_diff + b * cnt - m * sum_q_diff
        return cnt, s


def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, M, C, K = data[:4]
    A = data[4:4 + N]

    g = math.gcd(C, M)
    M1 = M // g

    if M1 == 1:
        print(K * min(A))
        return

    C1 = C // g
    d = (-C1) % M1

    mp = {}
    for x in A:
        q = x // g
        r = x % g
        if q not in mp or r < mp[q]:
            mp[q] = r

    qs = sorted(mp.keys())
    weights = [mp[q] for q in qs]
    P = len(qs)

    pc = PrefixCounter(K, M1, d, 0)
    prefix = pc.prefix

    total_cnt = K
    total_sum = pc.total_sum

    pref_cnt = []
    pref_sum = []
    for q in qs:
        c, s = prefix(q + 1)
        pref_cnt.append(c)
        pref_sum.append(s)

    delta_sum = 0
    rho_sum = 0

    # Non-wrap gaps
    for i in range(P - 1):
        L = qs[i]
        R = qs[i + 1]
        w = weights[i + 1]

        cnt = pref_cnt[i + 1] - pref_cnt[i]
        sumu = pref_sum[i + 1] - pref_sum[i]

        delta_sum += cnt * R - sumu
        rho_sum += cnt * w

    # Wrap gap
    L = qs[-1]
    R0 = qs[0]
    w0 = weights[0]

    # u in [L+1, M1)
    cnt1 = total_cnt - pref_cnt[-1]
    sum1 = total_sum - pref_sum[-1]
    delta_sum += cnt1 * (R0 + M1) - sum1
    rho_sum += cnt1 * w0

    # u in [0, R0]
    cnt2 = pref_cnt[0]
    sum2 = pref_sum[0]
    delta_sum += cnt2 * R0 - sum2
    rho_sum += cnt2 * w0

    ans = g * delta_sum + rho_sum
    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
